<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove Lech's multiplicity conjecture: Hilbert–Samuel multiplicity cannot decrease under a flat local homomorphism of nonzero Noetherian local rings. The result holds in arbitrary dimension, with no restrictions on the residue fields or characteristics.

- **C1** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Let $(R,\mathfrak m)\longrightarrow(S,\mathfrak n)$ be a flat local homomorphism of nonzero Noetherian local rings. Then $$e(R)\leq e(S).$$ There is no restriction on the dimensions, the residue fields, or the characteristics of the rings.
  - Explanation: Multiplicity cannot decrease under the stated flat local homomorphism.
- **C2** (bound, [source](#stmt-thm-complex)): Let $C$, $I$, and $\lambda$ be as above. Assume that $\lambda$ is additive on short exact sequences, that $\lambda(0)=0$, and that direct sums and filtered colimits of length-zero modules killed by a common power of $I$ have length zero. Assume also <a href="#eq:complex-length-hypotheses" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:complex-length-hypotheses">[eq:complex-length-hypotheses]</a>. Let $F$ be a complex of finite free $C$-modules, concentrated in degrees $0,\ldots,h$, which is contractible locally on $\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}C\setminus V(I)$. Set $b_i=\mathop{\mathrm{rank}}_C F_i$. Then $H_i(F)$ is annihilated by a power of $I$, $$\lambda H_i(F)=0\quad(i>0),\qquad \lambda H_0(F)<\infty.$$ Suppose further that $$\sum_{i=0}^h(-1)^i b_i=0 \quad\text{and that all differential entries belong to }I^s, \qquad s\geq1.$$ There is a constant $A_h$, depending only on $h$, such that $$\label{eq:complex-estimate} \lambda H_0(F)\ \geq\ \mu s^h\left(b_0-\frac{A_h}{s}\sum_{i=0}^h b_i\right).$$ In particular, for fixed $h$ and fixed ranks the error is $O_h(1/s)$, uniformly in $C$, $I$, $\lambda$, and $F$.
  - Explanation: The zeroth homology length has a uniform lower bound once the length stability, support, rank and differential hypotheses hold.
- **C3** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-acyclicity)): Under the standing length hypotheses, let $F$ be a finite free complex in degrees $0,\ldots,h$, contractible locally off $V(I)$. Each $H_i(F)$ is annihilated by a fixed power of $I$, all its lengths are finite, and its positive homology has length zero.
  - Explanation: Positive homology has length zero under the standing length hypotheses and local contractibility.
- **C4** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-complex-graded-comparison)): The graded surjection $$\label{eq:complex-graded-map} (C/I)[Z_1,\ldots,Z_h]\longrightarrow\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}_I C$$ has length-zero kernel in every degree. For every vector bundle $V$ on $\mathbb P_\mathbb Z^{h-1}$ and every integer $t$, restriction induces isomorphisms modulo length zero $$H^v(\mathbb P_{C/I}^{h-1},V(t))\longrightarrow H^v(E,V(t)).$$ All of these cohomology modules have finite length. Moreover, for $t\geq0$ the natural map $$\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}_I^t C\longrightarrow H^0(E,\mathcal O_E(t))$$ is an isomorphism modulo length zero, and $H^v(E,\mathcal O_E(t))$ has length zero for $v>0$.
  - Explanation: The graded comparison and induced bundle cohomology comparisons hold modulo length zero.
- **C5** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-complex-twist-maps)): Let $D$ be either $F_Y\otimes V$ or $G\otimes V$, with $V$ a vector bundle pulled back from $\mathbb P_\mathbb Z^{h-1}$. There is an integer $M_D$ such that, for every integer $j$ and every $M\geq M_D$, the inclusion $$D(j+M)\longrightarrow D(j)$$ induces zero on hyperhomology. All hyperhomology modules of $D(j)$ are annihilated by a power of $I$ and have finite length.
  - Explanation: Sufficiently deep twist inclusions act trivially on hyperhomology, whose lengths are finite.
- **C6** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-complex-euler-comparison)): One has $$\label{eq:complex-euler-comparison} \chi_\lambda(F_Y)=\sum_i(-1)^i\lambda H_i(F)=\lambda H_0(F).$$ If $\sum_i(-1)^i b_i=0$, then for every vector bundle $V$ pulled back from $\mathbb P_\mathbb Z^{h-1}$, $$\label{eq:complex-bundle-rank} \chi_\lambda(F_Y\otimes V)=(\mathop{\mathrm{rank}}V)\chi_\lambda(F_Y).$$
  - Explanation: The blowup Euler characteristic recovers the original homology length; the bundle rank formula additionally requires alternating rank zero.
- **C7** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-complex-root-bundle)): Given integers $r_1>\cdots>r_{h-1}$, there is a vector bundle $V$ of rank $(h-1)!$ on $\mathbb P_\mathbb Z^{h-1}$ with the following property. For every ring $A$ and every integer $a$, $V(a)$ on $\mathbb P_A^{h-1}$ is acyclic if $a=r_j$ for some $j$. Otherwise its cohomology is a finite free $A$-module in the single degree $$v(a)=\#\{j:r_j>a\},$$ and its Euler characteristic in ranks is $$\prod_{j=1}^{h-1}(a-r_j).$$ For $A=C/I$, the Euler characteristic with respect to $\lambda$ is $\mu\prod_j(a-r_j)$, and the same Euler characteristic and cohomology vanishing statements hold on $E$, modulo length zero.
  - Explanation: A bundle realizes the prescribed integer roots with cohomology concentrated in the stated degree.
- **C8** (construction, [source](#stmt-thm-length-inputs)): Let $(D,\mathfrak d,k)$ be a complete Noetherian local domain of dimension $h>0$, with perfect residue field $k$ of characteristic $p>0$. There exist a $D$-algebra $C$ and a function $\lambda$, with values in $[0,\infty]$, on $C$-modules annihilated by some power of $\mathfrak d$, with the following properties.* 1. *The function is additive on short exact sequences and vanishes on the zero module. Direct sums, sums, and directed colimits of modules of length zero have length zero whenever a fixed power of $\mathfrak d$ annihilates all modules under consideration.* 2. *For every system of parameters $z_1,\ldots,z_h$ of $D$ and every integer $a\geq 1$, $$\lambda\bigl(C/(z_1^a,\ldots,z_h^a)C\bigr) =a^h e((z_1,\ldots,z_h),D), \qquad \lambda H_i(z_1^a,\ldots,z_h^a;C)=0\quad(i>0).$$ In particular, the number $\mu=\lambda(C/(z_1,\ldots,z_h)C)$ is positive and finite.* 3. *If $H$ is $\mathfrak d$-primary, then $$\begin{cases} \lambda(C/HC)=e_{HK}(H,D),&\operatorname{char}D=p,\\ \lambda(C/HC)\leq e(H,D),&\operatorname{char}D=0. \end{cases}$$* *The same zero-length stability applies to modules killed by a fixed power of an ideal extended from any $\mathfrak d$-primary ideal of $D$.
  - Explanation: The auxiliary algebra supplies parameter lengths and zero-length Koszul homology; the primary quotient comparison depends on characteristic.
- **C9** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-normalized-limit)): Suppose that all the modules in the following assertions are annihilated by a fixed power of $\mathfrak m_A$.* 1. *Direct sums, sums, and directed colimits of zero-length modules have length zero.* 2. *If $M=\bigcup_n M_n$ is an increasing union of submodules, then $\lambda_\infty(M)=\sup_n\lambda_\infty(M_n)$.* 3. *If $M=\varinjlim_n M_n$ and $\lambda_\infty(M_n)\longrightarrow 0$, then $\lambda_\infty(M)=0$. No injectivity assumption on the transition maps is required in this assertion.
  - Explanation: The normalized length statements require a common annihilating power, including for noninjective colimit transitions.
- **C10** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-perfection-conductor)): There is an element $0\neq g\in A$ such that $$gC\subseteq B_1, \qquad g^{1/q}C\subseteq B_q\quad(q=p^n).$$
  - Explanation: A conductor and its roots control passage from perfection to finite root stages.
- **C11** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-perfection-finite-length)): In the characteristic-$p$ setup above, let $M$ be a finite-length $D$-module. If the limit on the right exists, then $$\lambda_\infty(C\otimes_D M) =\lim_{q=p^n\longrightarrow\infty} q^{-h}\mathop{\mathrm{\ell}}_D\bigl(\Phi_D^n(M)\bigr).$$
  - Explanation: The finite-length module identity assumes existence of the displayed limit.
- **C12** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-residue-extension)): For every complete Noetherian local ring $(B,\mathfrak b,k)$ and every algebraic closure $\overline{k}$ of $k$, there is a complete Noetherian local ring $B'$ and a flat local map $B\to B'$ such that $$\mathfrak bB'=\mathfrak b_{B'},\qquad B'/\mathfrak b_{B'}\cong\overline{k},\qquad e(B')=e(B).$$
  - Explanation: A flat local residue extension preserves maximal ideals and multiplicity.
- **C13** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-reduction)): To prove $e(R)\leq e(S)$ for all flat local maps of nonzero Noetherian local rings, it suffices to prove it when $R$ and $S$ are complete, $R$ is a domain, $\dim R=\dim S>0$, and the residue field of $S$ is algebraically closed. The reduction preserves equal characteristic $p$ when both original rings have characteristic $p$.
  - Explanation: The multiplicity inequality reduces to complete equal-dimensional rings with domain source and algebraically closed target residue field.
- **C14** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-setup)): Let $(R,\mathfrak m)\to(S,\mathfrak n)$ be a flat local map of complete Noetherian local rings. Suppose $R$ is a domain, $\dim R=\dim S=d>0$, the residue characteristic is $p>0$, and the residue field $L$ of $S$ is algebraically closed. There is a factorization $$R\longrightarrow (T,\mathfrak t)\longrightarrow S=T/J$$ with the following properties. Write $b=\dim(T/\mathfrak mT)$ and $h=d+b$.* 1. *The ring $T$ is complete, its residue field is $L$, and $R\to T$ is flat with regular closed fiber. Moreover $\dim T=h$ and $e(T)=e(R)$.* 2. *Every irreducible component of $T$ has dimension $h$. The ideal $J$ is perfect of grade $b$, and $S$ has a minimal finite free $T$-resolution $P_\bullet$ in degrees $0,\ldots,b$ with $P_0=T$.* 3. *Choose lifts $x_1,\ldots,x_d\in\mathfrak t$ of a minimal reduction of $\mathfrak n$. Then $$F_\bullet=P_\bullet\otimes_T K_\bullet(x_1,\ldots,x_d;T)$$ is a finite free complex in degrees $0,\ldots,h$, has $F_0=T$, and is contractible locally on $\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}T\setminus\{\mathfrak t\}$. Its differential entries lie in $\mathfrak t$, and its ranks have alternating sum zero.* 4. *If $Q\in\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}T$ contracts to zero in $R$, then $T_Q$ is a complete intersection. In mixed characteristic, $T_Q$ is regular. Every prime of $T$ which is minimal over $J$ contracts to zero in $R$. In mixed characteristic $T$ is generically reduced; a prime $Q$ minimal over $J$ contains a unique minimal prime of $T$.
  - Explanation: The factorization preserves source multiplicity and supplies a supported finite free complex, with the stated generic localization properties.
- **C15** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-ma-hk)): Let $(T,\mathfrak t)$ be a complete local ring of characteristic $p>0$ with perfect residue field, let $J$ be a perfect ideal, and put $S=T/J$. For any system of parameters $x_1,\ldots,x_d$ of $S$, $$e_{\mathrm{HK}}(J+(x),T) =\sum_{\substack{Q\text{ minimal over }J\\\dim(T/Q)=d}} e((x),T/Q)\,e_{\mathrm{HK}}(JT_Q,T_Q).$$ If each $T_Q$ occurring in this sum is a complete intersection, then $$e_{\mathrm{HK}}(J+(x),T)=e((x),S).$$
  - Explanation: The perfect-ideal identity compares Hilbert–Kunz multiplicity with the quotient parameter multiplicity under complete-intersection localization hypotheses.
- **C16** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-positive-characteristic)): For every flat local map $(R,\mathfrak m)\to(S,\mathfrak n)$ of nonzero Noetherian local rings of characteristic $p>0$, one has $e(R)\leq e(S)$.
  - Explanation: The multiplicity inequality holds for flat local maps in positive characteristic.
- **C17** (bound, [source](#stmt-cor-dutta-domain)): Let $(R,\mathfrak m)$ be a complete Noetherian local domain of characteristic $p>0$, and let $F$ be a short complex over $R$. Then $$\chi_\infty^R(F)\geq e(R).$$
  - Explanation: The Dutta multiplicity bound applies to short complexes over complete positive-characteristic local domains.
- **C18** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-mixed-quotient)): Let $(A,\mathfrak a)$ be a Noetherian local ring of positive dimension $t$, let $H$ be $\mathfrak a$-primary, and let $f\in H$. If $\dim A/(f)=t-1$, then $$e(H,A)\leq e(H,A/(f)).$$
  - Explanation: The primary multiplicity inequality requires the quotient dimension to drop as stated.
- **C19** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-mixed-asymptotic)): Let $(B,\mathfrak b)$ be a complete Noetherian local domain of dimension $h$, and let $1\leq d\leq h$. Suppose that an ideal $L\subseteq\mathfrak b$ and elements $x_1,\ldots,x_d\in\mathfrak b$ satisfy $\sqrt{L+(x_1,\ldots,x_d)}=\mathfrak b$. Put $$H_N=L+(x_1^N,\ldots,x_d^N),\qquad N\geq1.$$ The following assertions hold.* 1. *If $\dim B/L<d$, then $e(H_N,B)=O(N^{d-1})$.* 2. *If $L=\mathfrak q$ is prime, $\dim B/\mathfrak q=d$, and $B_{\mathfrak q}$ is regular, then $$e(H_N,B)\leq N^d e((x_1,\ldots,x_d),B/\mathfrak q) +O(N^{d-1}).$$* *All constants concern the fixed data $B,L,x$ and are independent of $N$.
  - Explanation: The parameter-power estimates distinguish small quotient dimension from a prime with regular localization.
- **C20** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-mixed-characteristic)): Assume the notation and conclusions of Proposition <a href="#prop:setup" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:setup">14</a>, and suppose that the source domain $R$ has mixed characteristic. Then $$e(S)\geq e(T)=e(R).$$
  - Explanation: The mixed-characteristic comparison holds within the factorization hypotheses.
- **C21** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-charzero-finite-free)): Let $(R,\mathfrak m)\to(S,\mathfrak n)$ be a flat local map between complete Noetherian local rings of equal dimension and residue characteristic zero. There is a factorization $$R\longrightarrow R'\longrightarrow S$$ such that $R'$ is complete local, $e(R')=e(R)$, and $R'\to S$ is finite free. Moreover, $R'$ and $S$ have the same coefficient field and the same residue field.
  - Explanation: The residue-characteristic-zero map factors through a multiplicity-preserving source extension with finite free target.
- **C22** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-charzero-certificate)): Let $(R_0,\mathfrak m_0)\to(S_0,\mathfrak n_0)$ be a finite free local map of complete local rings of dimension $d>0$, with a common infinite coefficient field $L$ and residue field $L$. Put $r=e(R_0)$. There is a finite system $$F(t_1,\ldots,t_d,Y_1,\ldots,Y_N)=0$$ of polynomial equations over $\mathbb Z$ with a solution in $L[[t_1,\ldots,t_d]]$, having the following property. For every field $K$ and every solution in $A=K[[t_1,\ldots,t_d]]$, the solution determines a finite free local map $$(R,\mathfrak a)\longrightarrow(S,\mathfrak b)$$ of nonzero complete local rings of dimension $d$ and residue field $K$, for which $$e(R)\ge r,\qquad e(S)\le e(S_0).$$
  - Explanation: A finite polynomial system preserves the source lower multiplicity bound and target upper multiplicity bound.
- **C23** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-charzero-specialization)): Let $L$ be a field of characteristic zero. A finite polynomial system over $\mathbb Z[t_1,\ldots,t_d]$ which has a solution in $L[[t_1,\ldots,t_d]]$ has a solution in $K[[t_1,\ldots,t_d]]$ for some algebraically closed field $K$ of positive characteristic.
  - Explanation: Existence of a power-series solution transfers to an algebraically closed field of positive characteristic.
- **C24** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-charzero)): For every flat local homomorphism of nonzero Noetherian local rings $(R,\mathfrak m)\to(S,\mathfrak n)$ whose residue fields have characteristic zero, one has $e(R)\le e(S)$.
  - Explanation: The multiplicity inequality holds when the residue fields have characteristic zero.

      "exact_statement": "One has $$\\label{eq:complex-euler-comparison}\n \\chi_\\lambda(F_Y)=\\sum_i(-1)^i\\lambda H_i(F)=\\lambda H_0(F).$$ If $\\sum_i(-1)^i b_i=0$, then for every vector bundle $V$ pulled back from $\\mathbb P_\\mathbb Z^{h-1}$, $$\\label{eq:complex-bundle-rank}\n \\chi_\\lambda(F_Y\\otimes V)=(\\mathop{\\mathrm{rank}}V)\\chi_\\lambda(F_Y).$$",
      "explanation": "The blowup Euler characteristic recovers the original homology length; the bundle rank formula additionally requires alternating rank zero.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-complex-root-bundle",
        "exact_quote": "Given integers $r_1>\\cdots>r_{h-1}$, there is a vector bundle $V$ of rank $(h-1)!$ on $\\mathbb P_\\mathbb Z^{h-1}$ with the following property. For every ring $A$ and every integer $a$, $V(a)$ on $\\mathbb P_A^{h-1}$ is acyclic if $a=r_j$ for some $j$. Otherwise its cohomology is a finite free $A$-module in the single degree $$v(a)=\\#\\{j:r_j>a\\},$$ and its Euler characteristic in ranks is $$\\prod_{j=1}^{h-1}(a-r_j).$$ For $A=C/I$, the Euler characteristic with respect to $\\lambda$ is $\\mu\\prod_j(a-r_j)$, and the same Euler characteristic and cohomology vanishing statements hold on $E$, modulo length zero.",
        "source_id": "canonical:PM-194.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

Otherwise its cohomology is a finite free $A$-module in the single degree $$v(a)=\\#\\{j:r_j>a\\},$$ and its Euler characteristic in ranks is $$\\prod_{j=1}^{h-1}(a-r_j).$$ For $A=C/I$, the Euler characteristic with respect to $\\lambda$ is $\\mu\\prod_j(a-r_j)$, and the same Euler characteristic and cohomology vanishing statements hold on $E$, modulo length zero.",
          "source_id": "canonical:PM-194.1"
        }
      ],

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-length-inputs",
        "exact_quote": "Let $(D,\\mathfrak d,k)$ be a complete Noetherian local domain of dimension $h>0$, with perfect residue field $k$ of characteristic $p>0$. There exist a $D$-algebra $C$ and a function $\\lambda$, with values in $[0,\\infty]$, on $C$-modules annihilated by some power of $\\mathfrak d$, with the following properties.*\n\n1.  *The function is additive on short exact sequences and vanishes on the zero module. Direct sums, sums, and directed colimits of modules of length zero have length zero whenever a fixed power of $\\mathfrak d$ annihilates all modules under consideration.*\n\n2.  *For every system of parameters $z_1,\\ldots,z_h$ of $D$ and every integer $a\\geq 1$, $$\\lambda\\bigl(C/(z_1^a,\\ldots,z_h^a)C\\bigr)\n       =a^h e((z_1,\\ldots,z_h),D),\n     \\qquad\n     \\lambda H_i(z_1^a,\\ldots,z_h^a;C)=0\\quad(i>0).$$ In particular, the number $\\mu=\\lambda(C/(z_1,\\ldots,z_h)C)$ is positive and finite.*\n\n3.  *If $H$ is $\\mathfrak d$-primary, then $$\\begin{cases}\n     \\lambda(C/HC)=e_{HK}(H,D),&\\operatorname{char}D=p,\\\\\n     \\lambda(C/HC)\\leq e(H,D),&\\operatorname{char}D=0.\n     \\end{cases}$$*\n\n*The same zero-length stability applies to modules killed by a fixed power of an ideal extended from any $\\mathfrak d$-primary ideal of $D$.",
        "source_id": "canonical:PM-194.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

          "source_id": "canonical:PM-194.1"

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-normalized-limit",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose that all the modules in the following assertions are annihilated by a fixed power of $\\mathfrak m_A$.*\n\n1.  *Direct sums, sums, and directed colimits of zero-length modules have length zero.*\n\n2.  *If $M=\\bigcup_n M_n$ is an increasing union of submodules, then $\\lambda_\\infty(M)=\\sup_n\\lambda_\\infty(M_n)$.*\n\n3.  *If $M=\\varinjlim_n M_n$ and $\\lambda_\\infty(M_n)\\longrightarrow 0$, then $\\lambda_\\infty(M)=0$. No injectivity assumption on the transition maps is required in this assertion.",
        "source_id": "canonical:PM-194.1"
      },

No injectivity assumption on the transition maps is required in this assertion.",
          "source_id": "canonical:PM-194.1"
        }

        "exact_quote": "For every complete Noetherian local ring $(B,\\mathfrak b,k)$ and every algebraic closure $\\overline{k}$ of $k$, there is a complete Noetherian local ring $B'$ and a flat local map $B\\to B'$ such that $$\\mathfrak bB'=\\mathfrak b_{B'},\\qquad\n  B'/\\mathfrak b_{B'}\\cong\\overline{k},\\qquad e(B')=e(B).$$",
        "source_id": "canonical:PM-194.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-residue-extension",

          "source_id": "canonical:PM-194.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For every complete Noetherian local ring $(B,\\mathfrak b,k)$ and every algebraic closure $\\overline{k}$ of $k$, there is a complete Noetherian local ring $B'$ and a flat local map $B\\to B'$ such that $$\\mathfrak bB'=\\mathfrak b_{B'},\\qquad\n  B'/\\mathfrak b_{B'}\\cong\\overline{k},\\qquad e(B')=e(B).$$",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-reduction",
        "exact_quote": "To prove $e(R)\\leq e(S)$ for all flat local maps of nonzero Noetherian local rings, it suffices to prove it when $R$ and $S$ are complete, $R$ is a domain, $\\dim R=\\dim S>0$, and the residue field of $S$ is algebraically closed. The reduction preserves equal characteristic $p$ when both original rings have characteristic $p$.",
        "source_id": "canonical:PM-194.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-reduction",
          "exact_quote": "To prove $e(R)\\leq e(S)$ for all flat local maps of nonzero Noetherian local rings, it suffices to prove it when $R$ and $S$ are complete, $R$ is a domain, $\\dim R=\\dim S>0$, and the residue field of $S$ is algebraically closed. The reduction preserves equal characteristic $p$ when both original rings have characteristic $p$.",
          "source_id": "canonical:PM-194.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "To prove $e(R)\\leq e(S)$ for all flat local maps of nonzero Noetherian local rings, it suffices to prove it when $R$ and $S$ are complete, $R$ is a domain, $\\dim R=\\dim S>0$, and the residue field of $S$ is algebraically closed. The reduction preserves equal characteristic $p$ when both original rings have characteristic $p$.",
      "explanation": "The multiplicity inequality reduces to complete equal-dimensional rings with domain source and algebraically closed target residue field.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-setup",
        "exact_quote": "Let $(R,\\mathfrak m)\\to(S,\\mathfrak n)$ be a flat local map of complete Noetherian local rings. Suppose $R$ is a domain, $\\dim R=\\dim S=d>0$, the residue characteristic is $p>0$, and the residue field $L$ of $S$ is algebraically closed. There is a factorization $$R\\longrightarrow (T,\\mathfrak t)\\longrightarrow S=T/J$$ with the following properties. Write $b=\\dim(T/\\mathfrak mT)$ and $h=d+b$.*\n\n1.  *The ring $T$ is complete, its residue field is $L$, and $R\\to T$ is flat with regular closed fiber. Moreover $\\dim T=h$ and $e(T)=e(R)$.*\n\n2.  *Every irreducible component of $T$ has dimension $h$. The ideal $J$ is perfect of grade $b$, and $S$ has a minimal finite free $T$-resolution $P_\\bullet$ in degrees $0,\\ldots,b$ with $P_0=T$.*\n\n3.  *Choose lifts $x_1,\\ldots,x_d\\in\\mathfrak t$ of a minimal reduction of $\\mathfrak n$. Then $$F_\\bullet=P_\\bullet\\otimes_T K_\\bullet(x_1,\\ldots,x_d;T)$$ is a finite free complex in degrees $0,\\ldots,h$, has $F_0=T$, and is contractible locally on $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Spec}}T\\setminus\\{\\mathfrak t\\}$. Its differential entries lie in $\\mathfrak t$, and its ranks have alternating sum zero.*\n\n4.  *If $Q\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{Spec}}T$ contracts to zero in $R$, then $T_Q$ is a complete intersection. In mixed characteristic, $T_Q$ is regular. Every prime of $T$ which is minimal over $J$ contracts to zero in $R$. In mixed characteristic $T$ is generically reduced; a prime $Q$ minimal over $J$ contains a unique minimal prime of $T$.",
        "source_id": "canonical:PM-194.1"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-ma-hk",
        "exact_quote": "Let $(T,\\mathfrak t)$ be a complete local ring of characteristic $p>0$ with perfect residue field, let $J$ be a perfect ideal, and put $S=T/J$. For any system of parameters $x_1,\\ldots,x_d$ of $S$, $$e_{\\mathrm{HK}}(J+(x),T)\n   =\\sum_{\\substack{Q\\text{ minimal over }J\\\\\\dim(T/Q)=d}}\n        e((x),T/Q)\\,e_{\\mathrm{HK}}(JT_Q,T_Q).$$ If each $T_Q$ occurring in this sum is a complete intersection, then $$e_{\\mathrm{HK}}(J+(x),T)=e((x),S).$$",
        "source_id": "canonical:PM-194.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-ma-hk",

          "source_id": "canonical:PM-194.1"
        }
      ],

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-mixed-quotient",

      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-mixed-asymptotic",
        "exact_quote": "Let $(B,\\mathfrak b)$ be a complete Noetherian local domain of dimension $h$, and let $1\\leq d\\leq h$. Suppose that an ideal $L\\subseteq\\mathfrak b$ and elements $x_1,\\ldots,x_d\\in\\mathfrak b$ satisfy $\\sqrt{L+(x_1,\\ldots,x_d)}=\\mathfrak b$. Put $$H_N=L+(x_1^N,\\ldots,x_d^N),\\qquad N\\geq1.$$ The following assertions hold.*\n\n1.  *If $\\dim B/L<d$, then $e(H_N,B)=O(N^{d-1})$.*\n\n2.  *If $L=\\mathfrak q$ is prime, $\\dim B/\\mathfrak q=d$, and $B_{\\mathfrak q}$ is regular, then $$e(H_N,B)\\leq N^d e((x_1,\\ldots,x_d),B/\\mathfrak q)\n                     +O(N^{d-1}).$$*\n\n*All constants concern the fixed data $B,L,x$ and are independent of $N$.",
        "source_id": "canonical:PM-194.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-mixed-asymptotic",
          "exact_quote": "Let $(B,\\mathfrak b)$ be a complete Noetherian local domain of dimension $h$, and let $1\\leq d\\leq h$. Put $$H_N=L+(x_1^N,\\ldots,x_d^N),\\qquad N\\geq1.$$ The following assertions hold.*\n\n1.  *If $\\dim B/L<d$, then $e(H_N,B)=O(N^{d-1})$.*\n\n2.  *If $L=\\mathfrak q$ is prime, $\\dim B/\\mathfrak q=d$, and $B_{\\mathfrak q}$ is regular, then $$e(H_N,B)\\leq N^d e((x_1,\\ldots,x_d),B/\\mathfrak q)\n                     +O(N^{d-1}).$$*\n\n*All constants concern the fixed data $B,L,x$ and are independent of $N$.",
          "source_id": "canonical:PM-194.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $(B,\\mathfrak b)$ be a complete Noetherian local domain of dimension $h$, and let $1\\leq d\\leq h$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-mixed-characteristic",
        "exact_quote": "Assume the notation and conclusions of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:setup\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:setup\">14</a>, and suppose that the source domain $R$ has mixed characteristic. Then $$e(S)\\geq e(T)=e(R).$$",
        "source_id": "canonical:PM-194.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Hilbert–Samuel multiplicity** ([source](#pm-h-2)): For a nonzero Noetherian local ring $(A,\mathfrak a)$ of dimension $d$, its Hilbert–Samuel multiplicity is $$e(A)=\lim_{N\longrightarrow\infty} \frac{d!\,\mathop{\mathrm{\ell}}_A(A/\mathfrak a^N)}{N^d}.$$
- **Ulrich module** ([source](#pm-h-4)): An Ulrich module is a nonzero finitely generated maximal Cohen–Macaulay module whose multiplicity equals its minimal number of generators.
- **Reduction of an ideal** ([source](#pm-h-10)): A *reduction* of an ideal $I$ is a subideal $Q$ such that $I^{n+1}=QI^n$ for all sufficiently large $n$; it is *minimal* if it is inclusion-minimal among reductions of $I$.
- **Isomorphism modulo length zero** ([source](#pm-h-12)): We say that a map between objects of $\mathscr T_I$ is an *isomorphism modulo length zero* if its kernel and cokernel have length zero.
- **Perfect ideal** ([source](#pm-h-25)): A *perfect ideal* $J$ of a Noetherian local ring $T$ means an ideal for which $T/J$ has finite projective dimension and $$\operatorname{pd}_T(T/J)=\operatorname{grade}_T J.$$
- **Short complex** ([source](#stmt-def-short-complex)): A *short complex* over $R$ is a complex of finite free modules $$F=(0\longrightarrow F_d\longrightarrow\cdots \longrightarrow F_0\longrightarrow0)$$ whose homology modules have finite length and for which $H_0(F)\ne0$.
- **Dutta multiplicity** ([source](#stmt-def-short-complex)): Its *Dutta multiplicity* is $$\chi_\infty^R(G)= \lim_{n\longrightarrow\infty} p^{-nd}\chi_R(\Phi_R^nG).$$
- **Hilbert–Kunz multiplicity** ([source](#sec:lengths)): Its Hilbert–Kunz multiplicity is $$e_{\mathrm{HK}}(H,D) =\lim_{q=p^n\longrightarrow\infty} \frac{\mathop{\mathrm{\ell}}_D(D/H^{[q]})}{q^{\dim D}}.$$

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **R1** [claim](#stmt-thm-complex) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-complex-root-bundle-2): Combine the blowup Euler comparison with prescribed-root bundles and layer sums. -> Uniform zeroth-homology length estimate
- **R2** [claim](#stmt-thm-length-inputs) / [proof](#pm-h-22): Construct normalized length algebras in the characteristic-dependent regimes. -> Parameter scaling and primary quotient comparisons
- **R3** [claim](#stmt-lem-reduction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-reduction): Complete, localize, filter by primes, and extend the target residue field. -> Complete domain source with equal dimensions and algebraically closed target residue field
- **R4** [claim](#stmt-prop-setup) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-setup): Use Cohen factorization and a parameter Koszul complex. -> Multiplicity-preserving auxiliary ring and supported free complex
- **R5** [claim](#stmt-thm-positive-characteristic) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-positive-characteristic): Raise differential entries by Frobenius, apply the complex estimate componentwise, and use the perfect-ideal identity. -> Positive-characteristic multiplicity inequality
- **R6** [claim](#stmt-cor-dutta-domain) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-dutta-domain): Use the finite-length-module comparison and Frobenius scaling, then pass through residue extension and prime filtration. -> Dutta multiplicity lower bound
- **R7** [claim](#stmt-thm-mixed-characteristic) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-mixed-characteristic): Deepen entries by integral extensions; compare normalized lengths with prime filtrations and take the limits in order. -> Mixed-characteristic multiplicity inequality
- **R8** [claim](#stmt-thm-charzero) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-charzero): Encode a hypothetical counterexample by finite polynomial identities and specialize to positive characteristic. -> Residue-characteristic-zero multiplicity inequality
- **R9** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-charzero-2): Assemble the characteristic cases through the reductions. -> Unrestricted multiplicity inequality

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **I-MULTIPLICITY**: Hilbert–Samuel multiplicity cannot decrease under the stated flat local homomorphism.
- **I-ROOT-PRODUCT**: The root-bundle twist is acyclic at a root; otherwise its cohomology is free in the specified single degree and its Euler characteristic is the product of the root differences.
- **I-LENGTH-STABILITY**: The complex estimate requires common-power stability for direct sums and filtered colimits of length-zero modules.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **AU-FLATNESS**: Assert multiplicity monotonicity for an arbitrary local map. -> The theorem supplies no such conclusion after flatness is removed.
- **AU-LENGTH**: Apply the complex estimate to any additive length. -> Additivity alone does not supply the required stability and Koszul conditions.
- **AU-CERTIFICATION**: Treat the finite root calculation as a proof of the multiplicity theorem. -> The calculation does not construct the blowup comparison or normalized length algebras.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. The prescribed-root construction has a finite cohomological product calculation. This implements that calculation, rather than the unrestricted multiplicity theorem or the construction of the vector bundle itself.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON list of records with integer twist a and descending integer roots.
# PRECONDITIONS: At most 3 roots in [-2,2]; twist in [-3,3]; strict descent; no booleans.
# OUTPUTS: JSON records: degree, dimension, Euler characteristic chi, and bundle rank.
# TIME: Linear arithmetic operations per root list; bounded batch of at most 200 records.
# SPACE: Linear in input and output batch size, with exact integer accumulators.
# ARITHMETIC: Certified exact arbitrary-precision fmpz integer arithmetic.
# FAILURE: Invalid records produce ERROR; malformed or oversized batches exit nonzero.
import sys, json
from flint import fmpz
def calculate(item):
    if not isinstance(item, dict) or set(item) != {"a", "roots"}:
        return {"error": "ERROR"}
    a, roots = item["a"], item["roots"]
    if type(a) is not int or not -3 <= a <= 3 or not isinstance(roots, list):
        return {"error": "ERROR"}
    if len(roots) > 3 or any(type(r) is not int or not -2 <= r <= 2 for r in roots):
        return {"error": "ERROR"}
    if any(roots[k] <= roots[k+1] for k in range(len(roots)-1)):
        return {"error": "ERROR"}
    chi = fmpz(1)
    for r in roots:
        chi *= a-r
    degree = None if a in roots else sum(r > a for r in roots)
    dimension = abs(chi)
    rank = fmpz.fac_ui(len(roots))
    return {"degree": degree, "dimension": int(dimension), "chi": int(chi), "rank": int(rank)}
batch = json.load(sys.stdin)
if not isinstance(batch, list) or not 1 <= len(batch) <= 200:
    sys.exit(2)
print(json.dumps([calculate(x) for x in batch], sort_keys=True))
```

    "oracle_code": "import itertools as it\nimport json as j\nimport sys as s\nimport math as m\ndef independent(record):\n    if type(record) != dict or sorted(record) != [\"a\", \"roots\"]:\n        return {\"error\": \"ERROR\"}\n    twist = record.get(\"a\")\n    rr = record.get(\"roots\")\n    if type(twist) != int or twist not in range(-3,4) or type(rr) != list:\n        return {\"error\": \"ERROR\"}\n    if len(rr) not in range(4) or any(type(z) != int or z not in range(-2,3) for z in rr):\n        return {\"error\": \"ERROR\"}\n    if rr != sorted(set(rr), reverse=True):\n        return {\"error\": \"ERROR\"}\n    factors = []\n    for z in rr:\n        line_twist = twist - 1 - z\n        if line_twist >= 0:\n            factors.append([(0,b) for b in range(line_twist+1)])\n        elif line_twist <= -2:\n            factors.append([(1,b) for b in range(-line_twist-1)])\n        else:\n            factors.append([])\n    dimensions = {}\n    for basis in it.product(*factors):\n        homological_degree = sum(entry[0] for entry in basis)\n        dimensions[homological_degree] = dimensions.get(homological_degree,0)+1\n    return {\"degree\": next(iter(dimensions)) if dimensions else None,\n            \"dimension\": sum(dimensions.values()),\n            \"chi\": sum((-1)**d*c for d,c in dimensions.items()),\n            \"rank\": m.factorial(len(rr))}\ntry:\n    entries = j.load(open(s.argv[1]))\n    truth = [independent(entry) for entry in entries]\n    actual = j.load(open(s.argv[2]))\n    wanted = j.loads(s.argv[3])\n    s.exit(0 if actual == truth and wanted == truth else 4)\nexcept Exception:\n    s.exit(5)\n",
    "output_contract": "Exact cohomological degree, dimension, Euler characteristic, and factorial bundle rank; degree is null at an acyclic twist.",
    "reason": "The prescribed-root construction has a finite cohomological product calculation. This implements that calculation, rather than the unrestricted multiplicity theorem or the construction of the vector bundle itself.",
    "reference_code": "# INPUTS: JSON list of records with integer twist a and descending integer roots.\n# PRECONDITIONS: At most 3 roots in [-2,2]; twist in [-3,3]; strict descent; no booleans.\n# OUTPUTS: JSON records: degree, dimension, Euler characteristic chi, and bundle rank.\n# TIME: Linear arithmetic operations per root list; bounded batch of at most 200 records.\n# SPACE: Linear in input and output batch size, with exact integer accumulators.\n# ARITHMETIC: Certified exact arbitrary-precision fmpz integer arithmetic.\n# FAILURE: Invalid records produce ERROR; malformed or oversized batches exit nonzero.\nimport sys, json\nfrom flint import fmpz\ndef calculate(item):\n    if not isinstance(item, dict) or set(item) != {\"a\", \"roots\"}:\n        return {\"error\": \"ERROR\"}\n    a, roots = item[\"a\"], item[\"roots\"]\n    if type(a) is not int or not -3 <= a <= 3 or not isinstance(roots, list):\n        return {\"error\": \"ERROR\"}\n    if len(roots) > 3 or any(type(r) is not int or not -2 <= r <= 2 for r in roots):\n        return {\"error\": \"ERROR\"}\n    if any(roots[k] <= roots[k+1] for k in range(len(roots)-1)):\n        return {\"error\": \"ERROR\"}\n    chi = fmpz(1)\n    for r in roots:\n        chi *= a-r\n    degree = None if a in roots else sum(r > a for r in roots)\n    dimension = abs(chi)\n    rank = fmpz.fac_ui(len(roots))\n    return {\"degree\": degree, \"dimension\": int(dimension), \"chi\": int(chi), \"rank\": int(rank)}\nbatch = json.load(sys.stdin)\nif not isinstance(batch, list) or not 1 <= len(batch) <= 200:\n    sys.exit(2)\nprint(json.dumps([calculate(x) for x in batch], sort_keys=True))\n",

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **L1** (hypothesis): The multiplicity comparison requires a flat local homomorphism of nonzero Noetherian local rings. Blocked use: ['Applying the inequality to arbitrary ring homomorphisms or software state transitions without an algebraic encoding.']
- **L2** (hypothesis): Additivity alone does not provide the length theory required by the complex estimate; stability under sums and filtered colimits with a common annihilating power is essential. Blocked use: ['Dropping the zero-length stability hypotheses for auxiliary rings.']
- **L3** (edge_case): Positive Koszul homology may have length zero without vanishing as a module. Blocked use: ['Replacing an isomorphism modulo length zero by an actual module isomorphism.']
- **L4** (hypothesis): The numerical lower bound for the complex requires alternating rank zero and differential entries in the stated ideal power. Blocked use: ['Applying the numerical estimate using support and freeness alone.']
- **L5** (approximation): The error constant depends only on the parameter count, but the homotopy exponents and truncation levels used for exact comparisons may depend on the auxiliary data. Blocked use: ['Interpreting the uniform numerical error as a uniform constructive bound for all proof operations.']
- **L6** (counterexample_scope): Passing to associated graded rings does not automatically preserve flatness. Blocked use: ['Transferring the flat-local theorem to associated graded maps without a separate flatness argument.']
- **L7** (counterexample_scope): The obstruction to nonzero finitely generated maximal Cohen–Macaulay modules does not rule out weakly lim Ulrich sequences. Blocked use: ['Conflating finite-module nonexistence with nonexistence of asymptotic sequences.']
- **L8** (hypothesis): The perfectoid length results are inputs; the auxiliary algebras are not asserted to be flat or Noetherian. Blocked use: ['Assuming tensoring with a length algebra is exact solely because that algebra is used in the proof.']
- **L9** (hypothesis): The Dutta-multiplicity corollary is stated for complete Noetherian local domains of positive characteristic. Blocked use: ['Claiming the full short-complex conjecture for all complete local rings.']
- **L10** (approximation): In mixed characteristic the parameter-power limit is taken for each fixed auxiliary extension before the depth parameter tends to infinity. Blocked use: ['Exchanging the limits or requiring a uniform parameter-power error across the varying extensions.']
- **L11** (hypothesis): The mixed-characteristic component sum counts each relevant filtration prime once because its localization is regular and lies on a unique component. Blocked use: ['Retaining the component-counting conclusion without its regularity and uniqueness hypotheses.']
- **L12** (nonconstructive): The specialization assertion supplies a positive-characteristic solution but does not specify a residue characteristic or an effective procedure for finding the solution. Blocked use: ['Treating existence of specialization as an executable algorithm with a specified characteristic.']
- **L13** (formal_scope): A finite computation of the cohomological degree and Euler product for prescribed integer roots checks that formula only; it does not certify existence of the vector bundle or the unrestricted multiplicity comparison. Blocked use: ['Publishing arithmetic checks as a certification of the whole paper.']

<a id="pm-formal"></a>
### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 194 (`lean/docs/194.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [Lech's multiplicity conjecture](../../preprints/Lechs-multiplicity-conjecture-September-23-2026/paper.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> Lech's multiplicity conjecture compares Hilbert–Samuel multiplicities across flat local maps. The linked formalization covers a supporting characteristic-$p$ comparison over a complete Noetherian local domain $D$: for a complex satisfying the stated short-complex and finite-length homology conditions, its Frobenius multiplicity sequence converges to its Dutta multiplicity, and the Hilbert–Samuel multiplicity of $D$ is at most that Dutta multiplicity.
>
> This selected statement is the complete-domain Dutta comparison. The paper's full flat-local result in arbitrary characteristic is outside it.

Comparator source `DuttaDomain.lean` (2645 bytes, sha256 `66c3e3c1254e9e71ac0c3a48c0aab3fe64a3c06e79cfdcf197bfd1e05fc8abb7`), verbatim:

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

noncomputable section
namespace Lech
noncomputable def dimension (A : Type*) [CommRing A] : ℕ :=
  (WithBot.unbotD 0 (ringKrullDim A)).toNat

noncomputable def colength (A : Type*) [CommRing A] [IsLocalRing A]
    (N : ℕ) : ℕ :=
  (Module.length A (A ⧸ (IsLocalRing.maximalIdeal A) ^ N)).toNat

noncomputable def normalizedColength (A : Type*) [CommRing A] [IsLocalRing A]
    (N : ℕ) : ℝ :=
  (Nat.factorial (dimension A) : ℝ) * (colength A N : ℝ) /
    (N : ℝ) ^ dimension A

noncomputable def multiplicity (A : Type*) [CommRing A] [IsLocalRing A] : ℝ :=
  Filter.limUnder Filter.atTop (normalizedColength A)
end Lech


 

noncomputable section
namespace Lech
open CategoryTheory CategoryTheory.Limits HomologicalComplex Filter
open scoped Topology
universe u
variable (R : Type u) [CommRing R] [IsNoetherianRing R] [IsLocalRing R]

 
structure IsShortComplex (F : CochainComplex (ModuleCat.{u} R) ℤ) : Prop where
  term_free : ∀ i, Module.Free R (F.X i)
  term_finite : ∀ i, Module.Finite R (F.X i)
  bounded : ∀ i, i < -(dimension R : ℤ) ∨ 0 < i → IsZero (F.X i)
  homology_finite_length : ∀ i, IsFiniteLength R (F.homology i)
  homology_zero_nonzero : ¬ IsZero (F.homology 0)

 

def frobeniusComplex (p : ℕ) [Fact p.Prime] [CharP R p] (n : ℕ)
    (F : CochainComplex (ModuleCat.{u} R) ℤ) : CochainComplex (ModuleCat.{u} R) ℤ :=
  ((ModuleCat.extendScalars (iterateFrobenius R p n)).mapHomologicalComplex (.up ℤ)).obj F

 
def shortEuler (F : CochainComplex (ModuleCat.{u} R) ℤ) : ℝ :=
  ∑ i∈Finset.range (dimension R+1),(-1:ℝ)^i*
    ((Module.length R (F.homology (-(i:ℤ)))).toNat : ℝ)

def duttaSequence (p : ℕ) [Fact p.Prime] [CharP R p]
    (F : CochainComplex (ModuleCat.{u} R) ℤ) (n : ℕ) : ℝ :=
  (p:ℝ)^(-(n*dimension R : ℤ)) * shortEuler R (frobeniusComplex R p n F)

def duttaMultiplicity (p : ℕ) [Fact p.Prime] [CharP R p]
    (F : CochainComplex (ModuleCat.{u} R) ℤ) : ℝ :=
  limUnder atTop (duttaSequence R p F)

 

def DuttaDomainClaim : Prop :=
  ∀ (D : Type u) [CommRing D] [IsNoetherianRing D] [IsLocalRing D] [IsDomain D]
    [IsAdicComplete (IsLocalRing.maximalIdeal D) D]
    (p : ℕ) [Fact p.Prime] [CharP D p]
    (F : CochainComplex (ModuleCat.{u} D) ℤ), IsShortComplex D F →
      (∀ n i,IsFiniteLength D ((frobeniusComplex D p n F).homology i)) ∧
      Tendsto (duttaSequence D p F) atTop (𝓝 (duttaMultiplicity D p F)) ∧
      multiplicity D ≤ duttaMultiplicity D p F
end Lech

namespace Lech
universe u
theorem dutta_domain : DuttaDomainClaim.{u} := by
  sorry
end Lech

end
end

end OAI
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove Lech's multiplicity conjecture: Hilbert–Samuel multiplicity cannot decrease under a flat local homomorphism of nonzero Noetherian local rings. The result holds in arbitrary dimension, with no restrictions on the residue fields or characteristics.

<a id="pm-h-1"></a>

| [stmt-lem-mixed-asymptotic](#stmt-lem-mixed-asymptotic) | lemma | `lem:mixed-asymptotic` | 21 | Let $(B,\mathfrak b)$ be a complete Noetherian local domain of dimension $h$, and let $1\leq d\leq h$. |
| [stmt-thm-mixed-characteristic](#stmt-thm-mixed-characteristic) | theorem | `thm:mixed-characteristic` | 22 | Assume the notation and conclusions of Proposition 14, and suppose that the source domain $R$ has mixed characteristic. |
| [stmt-lem-charzero-finite-free](#stmt-lem-charzero-finite-free) | lemma | `lem:charzero-finite-free` | 23 | Let $(R,\mathfrak m)\to(S,\mathfrak n)$ be a flat local map between complete Noetherian local rings of equal dimension and residue characteristic zero. |
| [stmt-lem-charzero-certificate](#stmt-lem-charzero-certificate) | lemma | `lem:charzero-certificate` | 24 | Let $(R_0,\mathfrak m_0)\to(S_0,\mathfrak n_0)$ be a finite free local map of complete local rings of dimension $d>0$, with a common infinite coefficient field $L$ and residue field $L$. |
| [stmt-lem-charzero-specialization](#stmt-lem-charzero-specialization) | lemma | `lem:charzero-specialization` | 25 | Let $L$ be a field of characteristic zero. |
| [stmt-thm-charzero](#stmt-thm-charzero) | theorem | `thm:charzero` | 26 | For every flat local homomorphism of nonzero Noetherian local rings $(R,\mathfrak m)\to(S,\mathfrak n)$ whose residue fields have characteristic zero, one has $e(R)\le e(S)$.* |

<a id="pm-h-2"></a>
### Introduction

For a nonzero Noetherian local ring $(A,\mathfrak a)$ of dimension $d$, its Hilbert–Samuel multiplicity is $$e(A)=\lim_{N\longrightarrow\infty}
       \frac{d!\,\mathop{\mathrm{\ell}}_A(A/\mathfrak a^N)}{N^d}.$$ In dimension zero this is simply the length of $A$. Multiplicity measures the leading-order growth of the infinitesimal neighborhoods of the closed point. Lech asked whether this invariant can decrease under a flat local homomorphism <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[Lech](#ref-Lech), [Lech64](#ref-Lech64)]. We prove that it cannot.

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1** (Lech’s conjecture). *Let $(R,\mathfrak m)\longrightarrow(S,\mathfrak n)$ be a flat local homomorphism of nonzero Noetherian local rings. Then $$e(R)\leq e(S).$$ There is no restriction on the dimensions, the residue fields, or the characteristics of the rings.*

</div>

Flatness compares the quotients by $\mathfrak m^N S$, whereas $e(S)$ is defined by the powers of $\mathfrak n$. Even when the closed fiber is zero-dimensional, so that both filtrations have finite colength, this difference prevents a direct comparison of their leading terms from giving the asserted lower bound for $e(S)$.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
###### Previous work.

Lech’s original work established the inequality when the source has dimension at most two and in important cases with complete-intersection closed fiber <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[Lech](#ref-Lech), [Lech64](#ref-Lech64)]. His original equal-dimensional formulation leads to the general one by the reductions recalled in Section <a href="#sec:reductions" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:reductions">4</a>. Ma proved the conjecture for equal-characteristic sources of dimension three and established the general bound $$e(R)\leq \max\{1,d!/2^d\}\,e(S),\qquad d=\dim R,$$ in equal characteristic <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[Ma](#ref-Ma), Theorems 1.2 and 1.3]. His use of Cohen factorizations and Hilbert–Kunz multiplicity is also central to the characteristic-$p$ argument here.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
###### Ulrich methods and their limits.

An Ulrich module is a nonzero finitely generated maximal Cohen–Macaulay module whose multiplicity equals its minimal number of generators. Such modules turn multiplicity into a generator count that can be compared across a flat map. Hanes developed this approach, including its asymptotic form: maximal Cohen–Macaulay modules of positive rank whose generator-to-multiplicity ratios tend to one also suffice. He obtained graded special cases by this method <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[Hanes](#ref-Hanes), Chapter IV, especially Proposition 4.1.5]. Ma’s weakly lim Ulrich sequences allow the modules themselves to fail to be Cohen–Macaulay: their multiplicity-to-generator ratios tend to one, while their first higher parameter Koszul Euler characteristics are negligible relative to the generator counts <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[MaUlrich](#ref-MaUlrich), Definitions 2.2 and 2.9]. For a local domain, existence of such a sequence implies the multiplicity inequality for every flat local target <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[MaUlrich](#ref-MaUlrich), Theorem 2.11].

In arbitrary dimension, Ma proved the conjecture when the source is a standard graded algebra over a perfect field, localized at its homogeneous maximal ideal; the flat local target need not be graded <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[MaUlrich](#ref-MaUlrich), Theorem 3.8]. The proof constructs weakly lim Ulrich sequences in positive characteristic and uses specialization in characteristic zero. Passing to associated graded rings does not extend this result to every flat map, because the induced map of associated graded rings need not remain flat <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[MaUlrich](#ref-MaUlrich), Remark 3.9]. A complementary graded case was proved by Meng: for an equal-dimensional flat local map, the inequality holds when, up to completion, the target is standard graded over a field and the extended source maximal ideal is homogeneous <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[Meng](#ref-Meng), Theorem 5.13].

Nor can one require weakly lim Ulrich sequences over every domain: Yhee constructed complete two-dimensional local domains admitting no such sequence <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[Yhee](#ref-Yhee), Theorem 6.2]. These examples are nonnormal. A different obstruction, within normal domains, is supplied by a companion construction of a complete three-dimensional Noetherian normal local $\mathbb C$-domain, with residue field $\mathbb C$, admitting no nonzero finitely generated maximal Cohen–Macaulay module <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[SmallCM](#ref-SmallCM), Theorem 1.1]. Thus approaches requiring such a module over every source ring itself cannot be universal; this finite-module obstruction does not rule out weakly lim Ulrich sequences.

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
###### From modules to complexes.

Over a $d$-dimensional local ring, a short complex is a finite free complex in degrees $0,\ldots,d$ with finite-length homology and nonzero zeroth homology. Iyengar, Ma, and Walker developed multiplicity and Betti-number inequalities for short free complexes using lim Ulrich methods <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[IMW2022](#ref-IMW2022)]. Their approach to Lech’s conjecture in <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[IMW2022](#ref-IMW2022), Proposition 8.3] uses a Cohen factorization and the tensor product of a finite free resolution with a parameter Koszul complex. This is the predecessor of the construction used here. They also used lim Ulrich sequences to compare cones of Betti tables with those over linear Noether normalizations <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[IMW2023](#ref-IMW2023)]. More recently, they related Ulrich modules to extensions of sheaves from the exceptional fiber of a blowup <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[IMW2025](#ref-IMW2025), Theorem 1.1], obtaining in dimension two short-complex inequalities under geometric hypotheses <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[IMW2025](#ref-IMW2025), Theorems 5.2 and 6.9]. These results motivate seeking positivity directly for complexes, without requiring Ulrich modules or sequences over every source.

Two difficulties remain in passing from such results to the unrestricted theorem: retaining coefficient $1$ in every dimension, and obtaining the required length estimates in mixed characteristic. Our argument uses a single estimate for free complexes in both settings. Its constants depend only on the number of parameters and the ranks, even when the auxiliary ring or its length function changes.

<a id="pm-h-6"></a>
###### A uniform estimate for complexes.

The main technical ingredient is Theorem <a href="#thm:complex" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:complex">2</a>. Let $I=(z_1,\ldots,z_h)C$ in a possibly non-Noetherian ring $C$, and suppose an additive length $\lambda$, satisfying the stability hypotheses stated there, makes the powers of $z$ behave as a parameter system: their positive Koszul homology has length zero and their quotient lengths scale as $a^h$. A finite free complex $F$ of length at most $h$ and alternating rank zero, contractible off $V(I)$, whose matrices have entries in $I^s$, satisfies $$\lambda(H_0F)\ \geq\ \lambda(C/I)s^h
       \left(\mathop{\mathrm{rank}}F_0-\frac{A_h\sum_i\mathop{\mathrm{rank}}F_i}{s}\right)$$ for sufficiently large $s$. The constant does not depend on the ring, the length theory, or any auxiliary finite extension.

Here $H_0F$ denotes the zeroth homology of $F$. The hypotheses on $\lambda$ are part of the estimate: additivity alone does not suffice. In particular, sums and filtered colimits of modules of length zero must still have length zero when one power of $I$ annihilates all the modules. Positive Koszul homology is required to have length zero, and need not itself vanish.

The proof combines a blowup comparison with the characteristic-free vector-bundle construction of Eisenbud–Schreyer <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[EisenbudSchreyer](#ref-EisenbudSchreyer)], which gives cohomology in a single degree. The relationship between free complexes, cohomology tables, and positivity has also been developed in the categorified duality of Eisenbud–Erman <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[EisenbudErman](#ref-EisenbudErman)]. Here the needed comparison of Euler characteristics is proved using finite filtrations and stabilized images of truncation maps. This comparison is needed because the ring $C$ need not be Noetherian. On the blowup, the differential entries can be divided by appropriate powers of the invertible ideal $I\mathcal O$. Tensoring the resulting complex with a suitable vector bundle places its global hyperhomology in degree zero, modulo length zero, and hence gives a nonnegative Euler characteristic. The bundle’s cohomological roots are the integer twists at which all its cohomology vanishes. These roots, after division by $s$, are chosen near the roots of the derivative of $\prod_{i=1}^h(x+i)$; this choice makes all the relevant layer sums have the same leading term. Here the layers are successive quotients for powers of the invertible ideal $I\mathcal O$ on the blowup. The alternating rank identity then leaves precisely the contribution of $F_0$.

The estimate applies to length theories beyond ordinary module length. For the present problem we use perfection in characteristic $p$ and the perfectoid normalized lengths of Cai–Lee–Ma–Schwede–Tucker in mixed characteristic <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[CLMST](#ref-CLMST)].

<a id="pm-h-7"></a>
###### A Dutta-multiplicity consequence.

In characteristic $p$, the same estimate also applies to arbitrary short complexes. For such a complex over a $d$-dimensional ring of characteristic $p>0$, its Dutta multiplicity <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[Dutta](#ref-Dutta)] is the Frobenius-normalized Euler characteristic $$\chi_\infty^R(F)=\lim_{n\to\infty}p^{-nd}
       \sum_i(-1)^i\mathop{\mathrm{\ell}}_R H_i(\Phi_R^nF),$$ where $\Phi_R^nF$ is obtained by raising the differential entries to their $p^n$th powers. Corollary <a href="#cor:dutta-domain" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:dutta-domain">19</a> proves $\chi_\infty(F)\geq e(R)$ for every short complex over a complete Noetherian local domain of characteristic $p>0$. This is the case of complete local domains of positive characteristic in the conjecture of Iyengar–Ma–Walker <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[IMW2022](#ref-IMW2022), Conjecture 8.1]. Their full conjecture, for all complete local rings, would imply Lech’s conjecture <a id="pm-cite-22"></a>[[IMW2022](#ref-IMW2022), Proposition 8.3]. Here the stated domain case is a consequence of the uniform estimate, not an assumption in the proof of Lech’s conjecture.

<a id="pm-h-8"></a>
###### How the estimate proves the conjecture.

After reductions that preserve multiplicity or use its additivity, we work with a complete domain source, equal source and target dimensions, and an algebraically closed target residue field. In positive residue characteristic, a Cohen factorization supplies a complete local ring $T$ and a perfect ideal $J$ such that $S=T/J$ and $e(T)=e(R)$. If $d=\dim S$ and $h=\dim T$, a finite free resolution $P$ of $S$ over $T$ has length $h-d$. Choose parameters $x_1,\ldots,x_d$ in $S$ whose ideal is a reduction of its maximal ideal, so its multiplicity is $e(S)$, and lift them to $T$. The complex $$F=P\otimes_T K(x_1,\ldots,x_d;T)$$ has length $h$, has degree-zero rank one, and is contractible away from the closed point. Here $K(x;T)$ is the Koszul complex. This is the complex to which the uniform estimate is applied.

The two positive-residue-characteristic arguments turn this complex into a multiplicity comparison in different ways. In characteristic $p$, Frobenius raises the differential entries to arbitrarily high order without changing their ranks. On each irreducible component of $T$, normalized length on the perfection identifies the length of the resulting zeroth homology with a Hilbert–Kunz multiplicity. The complex estimate gives a lower bound by the Hilbert–Samuel multiplicity of that component. Associativity and Ma’s perfect-ideal identity then identify the sum with $e(S)$.

In mixed characteristic, roots of parameters and Heitmann’s integral-extension Briançon–Skoda theorem <a id="pm-cite-23"></a>[[Heitmann](#ref-Heitmann)], in the form recalled in <a id="pm-cite-24"></a>[[HeitmannMa](#ref-HeitmannMa), Proposition 2.8], replace Frobenius. The extension has a rank that may grow as the entries become deeper. Dividing normalized lengths by that rank cancels it from the lower bound. For each fixed extension we obtain an upper bound by replacing $x_i$ with $x_i^N$ and taking $N$ to infinity in a prime filtration of $S$. Only after this limit do we let the depth of the differential entries tend to infinity. The upper bound is independent of the chosen extension. Moreover, each top-dimensional filtration prime lies on exactly one component of $T$, which is the reason this summation retains coefficient one.

In residue characteristic zero, we first replace the given map by a finite free map with a common coefficient field. A finite set of polynomial identities records algebra structures, freeness, locality, a lower bound on the source multiplicity, and an upper bound on the target multiplicity. The last two conditions use presentation minors and a reduction relation on the source, and monic initial relations for the target’s associated graded ring. Artin approximation <a id="pm-cite-25"></a>[[Artin](#ref-Artin)] and specialization preserve these identities in positive characteristic. A strict counterexample would therefore contradict the characteristic-$p$ case.

<a id="pm-h-9"></a>
###### Organization.

Section <a href="#sec:complex" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:complex">2</a> proves the uniform estimate. Section <a href="#sec:lengths" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:lengths">3</a> constructs its length inputs in positive and mixed characteristic. Section <a href="#sec:reductions" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:reductions">4</a> makes the flat-local reductions and constructs $T$ and $F$. Sections <a href="#sec:charp" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:charp">5</a> and <a href="#sec:mixed" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:mixed">6</a> prove the two cases of positive residue characteristic; Section <a href="#sec:charp" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:charp">5</a> also proves the Dutta-multiplicity corollary. Section <a href="#sec:charzero" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:charzero">7</a> gives the finite encoding and specialization in residue characteristic zero, and Section <a href="#sec:assembly" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:assembly">8</a> assembles the theorem.

<a id="pm-h-10"></a>
###### Conventions and background.

Rings are commutative with identity. Local rings are Noetherian unless explicitly introduced as auxiliary rings for an additive length; those auxiliary rings remain exempt from this convention when used in later sections. For an ideal of definition $H$ of a local ring $A$, write $e(H,A)$ for its Hilbert–Samuel multiplicity. A system of parameters has length $\dim A$. A *reduction* of an ideal $I$ is a subideal $Q$ such that $I^{n+1}=QI^n$ for all sufficiently large $n$; it is *minimal* if it is inclusion-minimal among reductions of $I$. For an ideal of definition, $e(Q,A)=e(I,A)$. Indeed, the reduction relation gives $I^{n+c}\subseteq Q^n\subseteq I^n$ for one fixed $c$ and all $n$, so their leading length coefficients agree. We use homological indexing: a sheaf-cohomology degree $v$ contributes total homological degree $i-v$ to a term of complex degree $i$. The shift $F[t]$ has $H_i(F[t])=H_{i-t}(F)$. We use the standard additivity and associativity formulas for multiplicity, and state the deeper literature inputs where they enter. In particular, the perfectoid length theorems are inputs, not claims that the auxiliary algebras are flat or Noetherian.

<a id="sec:complex"></a>
### A complex estimate with an additive length

The goal of this section is to bound the zeroth homology of a short free complex from below using only the order of its differential entries and the ranks of its terms. We first isolate the length hypotheses, then prove the comparison between the complex and its pullback to a blowup. A vector bundle with prescribed cohomological roots will turn that comparison into the required inequality.

<a id="pm-h-12"></a>
#### Length hypotheses and the estimate

All complexes in this section are indexed homologically. Thus a sheaf cohomology group in degree $v$, arising from the term of a complex in degree $i$, contributes to total degree $i-v$.

<a id="eq:complex-length-hypotheses"></a>
Let $C$ be a commutative ring, let $h\geq 1$, and put $I=(z_1,\ldots,z_h)C$. Write $\mathscr T_I$ for the abelian category of $C$-modules annihilated by some power of $I$. Suppose that $$\lambda:\mathscr T_I\longrightarrow [0,\infty]$$ is additive on short exact sequences and satisfies $\lambda(0)=0$. We assume, in addition, that direct sums and filtered colimits of length-zero modules annihilated by a common power of $I$ have length zero. The standing numerical and homological assumptions are $$\label{eq:complex-length-hypotheses}
 \begin{split}
 0<\mu&:=\lambda(C/I)<\infty,\\
 \lambda\bigl(C/(z_1^a,\ldots,z_h^a)C\bigr)&=a^h\mu
       \qquad(a\geq 1),\\
 \lambda H_i(z_1^a,\ldots,z_h^a;C)&=0
       \qquad(a\geq 1,\ i>0).
 \end{split}$$ No Noetherian hypothesis is imposed on $C$.

We say that a map between objects of $\mathscr T_I$ is an *isomorphism modulo length zero* if its kernel and cokernel have length zero. The length-zero objects form a Serre subcategory. Consequently one may work in the quotient by this subcategory when taking kernels, images, cokernels, or finite spectral sequences. In particular, a commutative square whose two vertical maps are isomorphisms modulo length zero induces such an isomorphism between the images of its horizontal maps. All uses of this convention below involve finite filtrations.

We will also use the following elementary observation about complexes with finite exhaustive filtrations in a common index range. If a filtration-preserving map induces zero on the first page of its spectral sequence, it induces zero on the associated graded of homology and hence lowers the filtration on homology by one. A composition of as many such maps as there are filtration steps is therefore zero on homology. The same statement holds modulo length zero whenever the pages under consideration belong to $\mathscr T_I$.

<a id="stmt-thm-complex"></a>
<div id="thm:complex" class="theorem">

<a id="eq:complex-estimate"></a>
**Theorem 2**. *Let $C$, $I$, and $\lambda$ be as above. Assume that $\lambda$ is additive on short exact sequences, that $\lambda(0)=0$, and that direct sums and filtered colimits of length-zero modules killed by a common power of $I$ have length zero. Assume also <a href="#eq:complex-length-hypotheses" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:complex-length-hypotheses">[eq:complex-length-hypotheses]</a>. Let $F$ be a complex of finite free $C$-modules, concentrated in degrees $0,\ldots,h$, which is contractible locally on $\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}C\setminus V(I)$. Set $b_i=\mathop{\mathrm{rank}}_C F_i$. Then $H_i(F)$ is annihilated by a power of $I$, $$\lambda H_i(F)=0\quad(i>0),\qquad \lambda H_0(F)<\infty.$$ Suppose further that $$\sum_{i=0}^h(-1)^i b_i=0
 \quad\text{and that all differential entries belong to }I^s,
 \qquad s\geq1.$$ There is a constant $A_h$, depending only on $h$, such that $$\label{eq:complex-estimate}
 \lambda H_0(F)\ \geq\
 \mu s^h\left(b_0-\frac{A_h}{s}\sum_{i=0}^h b_i\right).$$ In particular, for fixed $h$ and fixed ranks the error is $O_h(1/s)$, uniformly in $C$, $I$, $\lambda$, and $F$.*

</div>

<a id="pm-h-13"></a>
#### Koszul acyclicity modulo length zero

We first prove the homology assertion, which does not require the conditions on ranks or differential entries.

<a id="stmt-lem-acyclicity"></a>
<div id="lem:acyclicity" class="lemma">

**Lemma 3**. *Under the standing length hypotheses, let $F$ be a finite free complex in degrees $0,\ldots,h$, contractible locally off $V(I)$. Each $H_i(F)$ is annihilated by a fixed power of $I$, all its lengths are finite, and its positive homology has length zero.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-acyclicity"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-acyclicity</summary>

*Proof.* For each $j$, the complex $F[1/z_j]$ is exact. A bounded exact complex of projective modules is contractible, so clearing the finitely many denominators in a contracting homotopy shows that some power of $z_j$ acts nullhomotopically on $F$. Choose a common exponent $a_0$. It follows that $(z_1^{a_0},\ldots,z_h^{a_0})$ annihilates every $H_i(F)$, and therefore so does $I^{h(a_0-1)+1}$.

<a id="eq:complex-koszul-splitting"></a>
Put $K_a=K(z_1^a,\ldots,z_h^a;C)$. If $a\geq a_0$, each multiplication by $z_j^a$ is nullhomotopic on $F$ and also on its tensor product with any of the other Koszul factors. Splitting the resulting mapping cones gives $$\label{eq:complex-koszul-splitting}
 F\otimes_C K_a\simeq
 \bigoplus_{T\subseteq\{1,\ldots,h\}}F[|T|].$$ The splittings can be chosen to preserve the inclusion of the copy of $F$ in Koszul degree zero.

The homology of $F\otimes K_a$ is annihilated by $(z_1^a,\ldots,z_h^a)$, since these multiplications are nullhomotopic on $K_a$. In the bounded double-complex spectral sequence obtained by taking Koszul homology first, all positive Koszul rows have length zero by <a href="#eq:complex-length-hypotheses" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:complex-length-hypotheses">[eq:complex-length-hypotheses]</a>. The remaining row has terms $$F_i\otimes_C C/(z_1^a,\ldots,z_h^a),\qquad 0\leq i\leq h,$$ of finite length. Hence every $H_n(F\otimes K_a)$ has finite length, and its length is zero when $n>h$. The summand $F[h]$ in <a href="#eq:complex-koszul-splitting" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:complex-koszul-splitting">[eq:complex-koszul-splitting]</a> proves the assertions: $H_0(F)$ is a summand of $H_h(F\otimes K_a)$, and $H_i(F)$ for $i>0$ is a summand of $H_{h+i}(F\otimes K_a)$. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-14"></a>
#### The blowup and its exceptional divisor

The homology lengths are now finite. To estimate them, we pass to a space on which $I$ becomes invertible, while keeping track of the Euler characteristic of the original complex. Let $$Y=\mathop{\mathrm{Proj}}_C\Bigl(\bigoplus_{n\geq0}I^n\Bigr),\qquad
 L=I\mathcal O_Y=\mathcal O_Y(1),\qquad E=V(L).$$ Here $\mathcal O_Y(1)$ is the Rees-algebra twist. Indeed, the standard chart corresponding to $z_i$ has ring $C[I/z_i]\subseteq C[1/z_i]$. On this chart $I$ becomes $(z_i)$ and $z_i$ is a nonzerodivisor. Thus $L$ is an invertible ideal, and $E$ is an effective Cartier divisor, even when $C$ is not Noetherian. Empty charts may simply be omitted. The generators of $I$ give a closed immersion $Y\longrightarrow\mathbb P_C^{h-1}$, and $$E=\mathop{\mathrm{Proj}}(\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}_I C)\longrightarrow\mathbb P_{C/I}^{h-1}.$$ For a vector bundle $V$ on $\mathbb P_\mathbb Z^{h-1}$ we use the same symbol for its pullbacks to these schemes, and write $V(t)=V\otimes\mathcal O(t)$.

<a id="stmt-lem-complex-graded-comparison"></a>
<div id="lem:complex-graded-comparison" class="lemma">

<a id="eq:complex-graded-map"></a>
**Lemma 4**. *The graded surjection $$\label{eq:complex-graded-map}
 (C/I)[Z_1,\ldots,Z_h]\longrightarrow\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}_I C$$ has length-zero kernel in every degree. For every vector bundle $V$ on $\mathbb P_\mathbb Z^{h-1}$ and every integer $t$, restriction induces isomorphisms modulo length zero $$H^v(\mathbb P_{C/I}^{h-1},V(t))\longrightarrow H^v(E,V(t)).$$ All of these cohomology modules have finite length. Moreover, for $t\geq0$ the natural map $$\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}_I^t C\longrightarrow H^0(E,\mathcal O_E(t))$$ is an isomorphism modulo length zero, and $H^v(E,\mathcal O_E(t))$ has length zero for $v>0$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-complex-graded-comparison"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-complex-graded-comparison</summary>

<a id="eq:complex-power-colength"></a>
*Proof.* Set $J_a=(z_1^a,\ldots,z_h^a)$. Since $I^{h(a-1)+1}\subseteq J_a$, the $I$-adic filtration of $C/J_a$ is finite. There is a graded surjection $$(C/I)[Z_1,\ldots,Z_h]/(Z_1^a,\ldots,Z_h^a)
 \longrightarrow\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}_I(C/J_a).$$ The sum of the lengths of the homogeneous pieces on each side is $a^h\mu$. Additivity and nonnegativity imply that every homogeneous piece of its kernel has length zero. For a given degree $n$, choose $a>n$. The relations $Z_i^a$ have no effect in degree $n$, and $J_a\subseteq I^{n+1}$, so the degree-$n$ map is precisely <a href="#eq:complex-graded-map" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:complex-graded-map">[eq:complex-graded-map]</a>. This proves the first assertion. It also gives $$\label{eq:complex-power-colength}
 \lambda(C/I^M)=\mu\binom{M+h-1}{h}\qquad(M\geq1).$$

Write $P=(C/I)[Z_1,\ldots,Z_h]$ and let $N$ be the kernel in <a href="#eq:complex-graded-map" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:complex-graded-map">[eq:complex-graded-map]</a>. The degree-zero component of a homogeneous localization of $N$, as used on any standard projective chart or its intersections, is a filtered colimit of homogeneous pieces of $N$. All these pieces are annihilated by $I$ and have length zero. The assumed colimit property therefore gives length zero for every such localized component. Tensoring with the restriction of $V(t)$ preserves this property, since that restriction is finite projective over the chart ring. The finite standard affine Cech complexes for projective space and $E$ consequently have termwise length-zero kernels, and restriction is an isomorphism modulo length zero on their cohomology.

To check finiteness without a hypothesis on $C/I$, resolve $V(t)$ on $\mathbb P_\mathbb Z^{h-1}$ by finitely many finite sums of twists of the structure sheaf. Such a resolution exists by taking a finite graded free resolution of a finitely generated graded module representing $V(t)$ over $\mathbb Z[Z_1,\ldots,Z_h]$. This polynomial ring is regular of finite dimension and therefore has finite global dimension <a id="pm-cite-26"></a>[[Stacks](#ref-Stacks), Tags 07NF and 00OE]; its finitely generated graded projectives are graded free, as follows by lifting a homogeneous basis modulo $(Z_1,\ldots,Z_h)$ and applying graded Nakayama. The sheaf resolution is locally split because its final cokernel is a vector bundle, so it remains exact after arbitrary base change. The cohomology of every twist on $\mathbb P_{C/I}^{h-1}$ is finite free over $C/I$. A finite spectral sequence for the resolution proves the required finiteness. Finally, for $t\geq0$ the polynomial degree-$t$ piece is the space of global sections of $\mathcal O(t)$ and its higher cohomology vanishes. Combining these facts with <a href="#eq:complex-graded-map" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:complex-graded-map">[eq:complex-graded-map]</a> proves the last assertions. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-15"></a>
#### Euler characteristics on the blowup

We now compare the homology lengths of $F$ with those of its pullback to $Y$. We will also show that, when the alternating rank is zero, tensoring the pullback with a vector bundle multiplies its Euler characteristic by the bundle’s rank.

For a bounded complex $D$ of quasi-coherent sheaves on $Y$, write $$\mathbb H_k(Y,D)=H_k\mathbf R\Gamma(Y,D).$$ These groups can be calculated using the finite standard affine Cech cover, whose intersections are affine. Whenever they are $I$-power torsion of finite length, put $$\chi_\lambda(D)=\sum_k(-1)^k\lambda\mathbb H_k(Y,D).$$ Only finitely many degrees occur in our applications.

<a id="eq:complex-G"></a>
Pull $F$ back to $Y$ and denote the resulting complex by $F_Y$. If the differential entries belong to $I^s$, define a complex $G$ by $$\label{eq:complex-G}
 G_i=L^{-is}\otimes_C F_i=\mathcal O_Y(-is)^{b_i}.$$ Its differential is induced by that of $F_Y$ after inverting $L$. On a chart where $L=(z_j)$, the matrix in the bases $z_j^{-is}F_i$ is $z_j^{-s}d_i$, where $d_i$ is the matrix for $F$. It is regular because the entries of $d_i$ belong to $L^s$. The natural inclusions $F_i\to L^{-is}\otimes_C F_i$ give an inclusion of complexes $F_Y\subseteq G$.

In degree $i$, the quotient $G/F_Y$ has successive layers $\mathcal O_E(l)^{b_i}$ for $l=-is,\ldots,-1$. When the alternating rank is zero, these finite layer ranges will account, after the Euler comparison, for the entire difference between $\chi_\lambda(G\otimes V)$ and $(\mathop{\mathrm{rank}}V)\lambda H_0(F)$. We first justify the comparison for an arbitrary bundle $V$; the choice of $V$ that gives a nonnegative Euler characteristic will follow.

<a id="stmt-lem-complex-twist-maps"></a>
<div id="lem:complex-twist-maps" class="lemma">

**Lemma 5**. *Let $D$ be either $F_Y\otimes V$ or $G\otimes V$, with $V$ a vector bundle pulled back from $\mathbb P_\mathbb Z^{h-1}$. There is an integer $M_D$ such that, for every integer $j$ and every $M\geq M_D$, the inclusion $$D(j+M)\longrightarrow D(j)$$ induces zero on hyperhomology. All hyperhomology modules of $D(j)$ are annihilated by a power of $I$ and have finite length.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-complex-twist-maps"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-complex-twist-maps</summary>

*Proof.* On a standard affine chart $U_i$, $L$ is generated by the nonzerodivisor $z_i$. The chart complex of $D$ consists of finite projective modules and becomes contractible after inverting $z_i$: away from $E$, both $F_Y$ and $G$ identify with the pullback of $F$. Clearing denominators in a contracting homotopy gives an exponent $m_i$ for which multiplication by $z_i^{m_i}$ is nullhomotopic on this chart. Trivializing $L$ shows that the same exponent works for every twist. It also works on all intersections contained in this chart. Choose $m$ at least as large as every $m_i$.

The map $D(j+m)\to D(j)$ is zero on the internal-homology page of the Cech double complex, since on each intersection it is identified with a nullhomotopic multiplication by a local equation to the power $m$. The Cech filtration has at most $h$ steps. The filtered-complex observation above therefore shows that $D(j+hm)\to D(j)$ is zero on hyperhomology. Every larger twist map factors through one of these, so $M_D=hm$ works.

For $a\in I^{M_D}$, multiplication by $a$ on $D(j)$ factors as $$D(j)\xrightarrow{a}D(j+M_D)\longrightarrow D(j).$$ Thus $I^{M_D}$ annihilates its hyperhomology. If $M\geq M_D$, the long exact sequence for $$0\longrightarrow D(j+M)\longrightarrow D(j)
 \longrightarrow D(j)/D(j+M)\longrightarrow0$$ injects $\mathbb H_k(Y,D(j))$ into the hyperhomology of the quotient. That quotient has a finite filtration by $E$-layers, each of whose terms is a finite sum of bundles $V(t)|_E$. Their sheaf cohomology has finite length by Lemma <a href="#lem:complex-graded-comparison" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:complex-graded-comparison">4</a>. The two finite spectral sequences, first for each layer and then for the filtration, give finite length for the quotient’s hyperhomology and hence for that of $D(j)$. ◻

</details>

The Euler characteristics of finite thickenings need not recover that of $F$. For example, in the alternating-rank-zero case needed for the estimate, $$\sum_k(-1)^k\lambda H_k(F/I^M F)
 =\lambda(C/I^M)\sum_i(-1)^i b_i=0.$$ Instead, we compare the images of homology maps from a sufficiently large thickening to a fixed smaller one. These images will recover the homology of $F$ and of $F_Y$, modulo length zero. This argument gives the first assertion below without the alternating-rank hypothesis; that hypothesis is used only afterward to prove invariance under twists and the vector-bundle formula.

<a id="stmt-lem-complex-euler-comparison"></a>
<div id="lem:complex-euler-comparison" class="lemma">

<a id="eq:complex-euler-comparison"></a>
<a id="eq:complex-bundle-rank"></a>
**Lemma 6**. *One has $$\label{eq:complex-euler-comparison}
 \chi_\lambda(F_Y)=\sum_i(-1)^i\lambda H_i(F)=\lambda H_0(F).$$ If $\sum_i(-1)^i b_i=0$, then for every vector bundle $V$ pulled back from $\mathbb P_\mathbb Z^{h-1}$, $$\label{eq:complex-bundle-rank}
 \chi_\lambda(F_Y\otimes V)=(\mathop{\mathrm{rank}}V)\chi_\lambda(F_Y).$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-complex-euler-comparison"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-complex-euler-comparison</summary>

<a id="eq:complex-thick-comparison"></a>
*Proof.* *Comparison on finite thickenings.* For each $M\geq1$ there is a natural comparison $$\label{eq:complex-thick-comparison}
 F/I^M F\longrightarrow
 \mathbf R\Gamma(Y,F_Y/L^M F_Y).$$ Filter its two sides by powers of $I$ and $L$, respectively. At the $l$th layer, $0\leq l<M$, comparison for each free summand is $$\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}_I^l C\longrightarrow\mathbf R\Gamma(E,\mathcal O_E(l)).$$ Lemma <a href="#lem:complex-graded-comparison" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:complex-graded-comparison">4</a> says that this is an isomorphism on homology modulo length zero. The finite filtration, together with the finite complex $F$, proves the same assertion for <a href="#eq:complex-thick-comparison" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:complex-thick-comparison">[eq:complex-thick-comparison]</a>. These comparisons commute with all transition maps as $M$ increases.

<a id="eq:complex-thick-triangle"></a>
<a id="eq:complex-geometric-image"></a>
*The geometric tower.* We identify the eventual images of these two towers. On the geometric side put $$A=\mathbf R\Gamma(Y,F_Y),\qquad
